# Rails engine namespace routing

## User

Explain the lifecycle and externally visible routing effects of `Rails::Engine.isolate_namespace`. Follow `engine_name`, `isolated?`, namespace table-name prefix behavior, `railtie_routes_url_helpers`, default `routes`/custom `endpoint`, mount helpers, `main_app`, controller lookup, and route-set creation through actual Railties code and engine/integration tests. Cover anonymous or unnamed namespaces, an explicitly assigned `engine_name`, an engine with no route file, and lazy versus eager route loading. Cite concrete source and test evidence, separate class-level setup from request-time dispatch, and do not propose changes.

When you call send_thread_message to report back, include the same findings in your final response in this thread; do not end with only a delivery acknowledgment. (or in your +Gemfile+), and it will automatically load models, controllers, and helpers\n  # inside +app+, load routes at <tt>config/routes.rb</tt>, load locales at\n  # <tt>config/locales/**/*</tt>, and load tasks at <tt>lib/tasks/**/*</tt>.\n  #\n  # == Configuration\n  #\n  # Like railties, engines can access a config object which contains configuration shared by\n  # all railties and the application.\n  # Additionally, each engine can access <tt>autoload_paths</tt>, <tt>eager_load_paths</tt> and\n  # <tt>autoload_once_paths</tt> settings which are scoped to that engine.\n  #\n  #   class MyEngine < Rails::Engine\n  #     # Add a load path for this specific Engine\n  #     config.autoload_paths << File.expand_path(\"lib/some/path\", __dir__)\n  #\n  #     initializer \"my_engine.add_middleware\" do |app|\n  #       app.middleware.use MyEngine::Middleware\n  #     end\n  #   end\n  #\n  # == Generators\n  #\n  # You can set up generators for engines with <tt>config.generators</tt> method:\n  #\n  #   class MyEngine < Rails::Engine\n  #     config.generators do |g|\n  #       g.orm             :active_record\n  #       g.template_engine :erb\n  #       g.test_framework  :test_unit\n  #     end\n  #   end\n  #\n  # You can also set generators for an application by using <tt>config.app_generators</tt>:\n  #\n  #   class MyEngine < Rails::Engine\n  #     # note that you can also pass block to app_generators in the same way you\n  #     # can pass it to generators method\n  #     config.app_generators.orm :datamapper\n  #   end\n  #\n  # == Paths\n  #\n  # Applications and engines have flexible path configuration, meaning that you\n  # are not required to place your controllers at <tt>app/controllers</tt>, but\n  # in any place which you find convenient.\n  #\n  # For example, let's suppose you want to place your controllers in <tt>lib/controllers</tt>.\n  # You can set that as an option:\n  #\n  #   class MyEngine < Rails::Engine\n  #     paths[\"app/controllers\"] = \"lib/controllers\"\n  #   end\n  #\n  # You can also have your controllers loaded from both <tt>app/controllers</tt> and\n  # <tt>lib/controllers</tt>:\n  #\n  #   class MyEngine < Rails::Engine\n  #     paths[\"app/controllers\"] << \"lib/controllers\"\n  #   end\n  #\n  # The available paths in an engine are:\n  #\n  #   class MyEngine < Rails::Engine\n  #     paths[\"app\"]                 # => [\"app\"]\n  #     paths[\"app/controllers\"]     # => [\"app/controllers\"]\n  #     paths[\"app/helpers\"]         # => [\"app/helpers\"]\n  #     paths[\"app/models\"]          # => [\"app/models\"]\n  #     paths[\"app/views\"]           # => [\"app/views\"]\n  #     paths[\"lib\"]                 # => [\"lib\"]\n  #     paths[\"lib/tasks\"]           # => [\"lib/tasks\"]\n  #     paths[\"config\"]              # => [\"config\"]\n  #     paths[\"config/initializers\"] # => [\"config/initializers\"]\n  #     paths[\"config/locales\"]      # => [\"config/locales\"]\n  #     paths[\"config/routes.rb\"]    # => [\"config/routes.rb\"]\n  #   end\n  #\n  # The <tt>Application</tt> class adds a couple more paths to this set. And as in your\n  # <tt>Application</tt>, all folders under +app+ are automatically added to the load path.\n  # If you have an <tt>app/services</tt> folder for example, it will be added by default.\n  #\n  # == Endpoint\n  #\n  # An engine can also be a Rack application. It can be useful if you have a Rack application that\n  # you would like to provide with some of the +Engine+'s features.\n  #\n  # To do that, use the ::endpoint method:\n  #\n  #   module MyEngine\n  #     class Engine < Rails::Engine\n  #       endpoint MyRackApplication\n  #     end\n  #   end\n  #\n  # Now you can mount your engine in application's routes:\n  #\n  #   Rails.application.routes.draw do\n  #     mount MyEngine::Engine => \"/engine\"\n  #   end\n  #\n  # == Middleware stack\n  #\n  # As an engine can now be a Rack endpoint, it can also have a middleware\n  # stack. The usage is exactly the same as in <tt>Application</tt>:\n  #\n  #   module MyEngine\n  #     class Engine < Rails::Engine\n  #       middleware.use SomeMiddleware\n  #     end\n  #   end\n  #\n  # == Routes\n  #\n  # If you don't specify an endpoint, routes will be used as the default\n  # endpoint. You can use them just like you use an application's routes:\n  #\n  #   # ENGINE/config/routes.rb\n  #   MyEngine::Engine.routes.draw do\n  #     get \"/\" => \"posts#index\"\n  #   end\n  #\n  # == Mount priority\n  #\n  # Note that now there can be more than one router in your application, and it's better to avoid\n  # passing requests through many routers. Consider this situation:\n  #\n  #   Rails.application.routes.draw do\n  #     mount MyEngine::Engine => \"/blog\"\n  #     get \"/blog/omg\" => \"main#omg\"\n  #   end\n  #\n  # +MyEngine+ is mounted at <tt>/blog</tt>, and <tt>/blog/omg</tt> points to application's\n  # controller. In such a situation, requests to <tt>/blog/omg</tt> will go through +MyEngine+,\n  # and if there is no such route in +Engine+'s routes, it will be dispatched to <tt>main#omg</tt>.\n  # It's much better to swap that:\n  #\n  #   Rails.application.routes.draw do\n  #     get \"/blog/omg\" => \"main#omg\"\n  #     mount MyEngine::Engine => \"/blog\"\n  #   end\n  #\n  # Now, +Engine+ will get only requests that were not handled by +Application+.\n  #\n  # == Engine name\n  #\n  # There are some places where an Engine's name is used:\n  #\n  # * routes: when you mount an Engine with <tt>mount(MyEngine::Engine => '/my_engine')</tt>,\n  #   it's used as default <tt>:as</tt> option\n  # * rake task for installing migrations <tt>my_engine:install:migrations</tt>\n  #\n  # Engine name is set by default based on class name. For +MyEngine::Engine+ it will be\n  # <tt>my_engine_engine</tt>. You can change it manually using the <tt>engine_name</tt> method:\n  #\n  #   module MyEngine\n  #     class Engine < Rails::Engine\n  #       engine_name \"my_engine\"\n  #     end\n  #   end\n  #\n  # == Isolated Engine\n  #\n  # Normally when you create controllers, helpers, and models inside an engine, they are treated\n  # as if they were created inside the application itself. This means that all helpers and\n  # named routes from the application will be available to your engine's controllers as well.\n  #\n  # However, sometimes you want to isolate your engine from the application, especially if your engine\n  # has its own router. To do that, you simply need to call ::isolate_namespace. This method requires\n  # you to pass a module where all your controllers, helpers, and models should be nested to:\n  #\n  #   module MyEngine\n  #     class Engine < Rails::Engine\n  #       isolate_namespace MyEngine\n  #     end\n  #   end\n  #\n  # With such an engine, everything that is inside the +MyEngine+ module will be isolated from\n  # the application.\n  #\n  # Consider this controller:\n  #\n  #   module MyEngine\n  #     class FooController < ActionController::Base\n  #     end\n  #   end\n  #\n  # If the +MyEngine+ engine is marked as isolated, +FooController+ only has\n  # access to helpers from +MyEngine+, and <tt>url_helpers</tt> from\n  # <tt>MyEngine::Engine.routes</tt>.\n  #\n  # The next thing that changes in isolated engines is the behavior of routes.\n  # Normally, when you namespace your controllers, you also need to namespace\n  # the related routes. With an isolated engine, the engine's namespace is\n  # automatically applied, so you don't need to specify it explicitly in your\n  # routes:\n  #\n  #   MyEngine::Engine.routes.draw do\n  #     resources :articles\n  #   end\n  #\n  # If +MyEngine+ is isolated, the routes above will point to\n  # +MyEngine::ArticlesController+. You also don't need to use longer\n  # URL helpers like +my_engine_articles_path+. Instead, you should simply use\n  # +articles_path+, like you would do with your main application.\n  #\n  # To make this behavior consistent with other parts of the framework,\n  # isolated engines also have an effect on ActiveModel::Naming. In a\n  # normal \\Rails app, when you use a namespaced model such as\n  # +Namespace::Article+, ActiveModel::Naming will generate\n  # names with the prefix \"namespace\". In an isolated engine, the prefix will\n  # be omitted in URL helpers and form fields, for convenience.\n  #\n  #   polymorphic_url(MyEngine::Article.new)\n  #   # => \"articles_path\" # not \"my_engine_articles_path\"\n  #\n  #   form_with(model: MyEngine::Article.new) do\n  #     text_field :title # => <input type=\"text\" name=\"article[title]\" id=\"article_title\" />\n  #   end\n  #\n",

If a block is given to\n    # routes, it is appended to the engine.\n    def routes(&block)\n      if block_given?\n        if @route_blocks\n          @route_blocks << block\n        else\n          @routes ||= config.route_set_class.new_with_config(config)\n          @routes.append(&block)\n        end\n      elsif @routes.nil?\n        @routes = config.route_set_class.new_with_config(config)\n        if @route_blocks\n          blocks, @route_blocks = @route_blocks, nil\n          blocks.each { |b| routes(&b) }\n        end\n      end\n      @routes\n    end\n\n    # Define the configuration object for the engine.\n    def config\n      @config ||= Engine::Configuration.new(self.class.find_root(self.class.called_from))\n    end\n\n    # Load data from db/seeds.rb file. :anchor\n            raise ArgumentError, \"anchor is ignored unless passed to `match`\"\n          end\n\n          @scope.options.each do |option|\n            if option == :blocks\n              value = block\n            elsif option == :options\n              value = options\n            else\n              value = options.delete(option) { POISON }\n            end\n\n            unless POISON == value\n              scope[option] = send(\"merge_#{option}_scope\", @scope[option], value)\n            end\n          end\n\n          @scope = @scope.new scope\n          yield\n          self\n        ensure\n          @scope = @scope.parent\n        end\n\n        POISON = Object.new.freeze # :nodoc:\n\n        # Scopes routes to a specific controller\n        #\n        #     controller \"food\" do\n        #       match \"bacon\", action: :bacon, via: :get\n        #     end\n        def controller(controller)\n          @scope = @scope.new(controller: controller)\n          yield\n        ensure\n          @scope = @scope.parent\n        end\n\n        # Scopes routes to a specific namespace. For example:\n        #\n        #     namespace :admin do\n        #       resources :posts\n        #     end\n        #\n        # This generates the following routes:\n        #\n        #         admin_posts GET       /admin/posts(.:format)          admin/posts#index\n        #         admin_posts POST      /admin/posts(.:format)          admin/posts#create\n        #      new_admin_post GET       /admin/posts/new(.:format)      admin/posts#new\n        #     edit_admin_post GET       /admin/posts/:id/edit(.:format) admin/posts#edit\n        #          admin_post GET       /admin/posts/:id(.:format)      admin/posts#show\n        #          admin_post PATCH/PUT /admin/posts/:id(.:format)      admin/posts#update\n        #          admin_post DELETE    /admin/posts/:id(.:format)      admin/posts#destroy\n        #\n        # ### Options\n        #\n        # The `:path`, `:as`, `:module`, `:shallow_path`, and `:shallow_prefix` options\n        # all default to the name of the namespace.\n        #\n        # For options, see `Base#match`. For `:shallow_path` option, see\n        # `Resources#resources`.\n        #\n        #     # accessible through /sekret/posts rather than /admin/posts\n        #     namespace :admin, path: \"sekret\" do\n        #       resources :posts\n        #     end\n        #\n        #     # maps to Sekret::PostsController rather than Admin::PostsController\n        #     namespace :admin, module: \"sekret\" do\n        #       resources :posts\n        #     end\n        #\n        #     # generates sekret_posts_path rather than admin_posts_path\n        #     namespace :admin, as: \"sekret\" do\n        #       resources :posts\n        #     end\n        def namespace(name, deprecated_options = nil, as: DEFAULT, path: DEFAULT, shallow_path: DEFAULT, shallow_prefix: DEFAULT, **options, &block)\n          if deprecated_options.is_a?(Hash)\n            as = assign_deprecated_option(deprecated_options, :as, :namespace) if deprecated_options.key?(:as)\n            path = assign_deprecated_option(deprecated_options, :path, :namespace) if deprecated_options.key?(:path)\n            shallow_path = assign_deprecated_option(deprecated_options, :shallow_path, :namespace) if deprecated_options.key?(:shallow_path)\n            shallow_prefix = assign_deprecated_option(deprecated_options, :shallow_prefix, :namespace) if deprecated_options.key?(:shallow_prefix)\n            assign_deprecated_options(deprecated_options, options, :namespace)\n          end\n\n          name = name.to_s\n          options[:module] ||= name\n          as = name if as == DEFAULT\n          path = name if path == DEFAULT\n          shallow_path = path if shallow_path == DEFAULT\n          shallow_prefix = as if shallow_prefix == DEFAULT\n\n          path_scope(path) do\n            scope(**options, as:, shallow_path:, shallow_prefix:, &block)\n          end\n        end\n\n        # ### Parameter Restriction\n        # Allows you to constrain the nested routes based on a set of rules. Otherwise, we pass the invalid name forward so the underlying\n              # router engine treats it and raises an exception.\n              if as == DEFAULT\n                candidate unless !candidate.match?(/\\A[_a-z]/i) || has_named_route?(candidate)\n              else\n                candidate\n              end\n            end\n          end\n\n          def set_member_mappings_for_resource # :doc:\n            member do\n              get :edit if parent_resource.actions.include?(:edit)\n              get :show if parent_resource.actions.include?(:show)\n              if parent_resource.actions.include?(:update)\n                patch :update\n                put   :update\n              end\n              delete :destroy if parent_resource.actions.include?(:destroy)\n            end\n          end\n\n          def api_only? # :doc:\n            @set.api_only?\n          end\n\n          def path_scope(path)\n            @scope = @scope.new(path: merge_path_scope(@scope[:path], path))\n            yield\n          ensure\n            @scope = @scope.parent\n          end\n",

```json
{
  "thread": "T-01a06130-c887-765b-8c03-b8a9325c590a",
  "message": "# `Rails::Engine.isolate_namespace`: lifecycle and routing effects\n\n`isolate_namespace` is class-level setup. It does not dispatch requests. It stamps the engine and the passed module so later boot, route compile, helper inclusion, and request dispatch behave as an isolated app. Mounting and `call` are a later phase.\n\n## 1. Class-level setup\n\n`Rails::Engine.isolate_namespace` lives on the engine class (`railties/lib/rails/engine.rb` 384–419). `Rails::Application#isolate_namespace` forwards to that class method (`railties/lib/rails/application.rb` 390–392).\n\nOn the **engine class** it always:\n\n1. Sets `engine_name` from the **module** name, not the engine class name: `engine_name(generate_railtie_name(mod.name))`.\n2. Sets `config.default_scope = { module: ActiveSupport::Inflector.underscore(mod.name) }`.\n3. Sets `self.isolated = true`.\n\n`engine_name` is an alias of `railtie_name` (`engine.rb` 356). `railtie_name` is a writer/reader (`railties/lib/rails/railtie.rb` 178–181). `generate_railtie_name` is `underscore(string).tr(\"/\", \"_\")` (`railtie.rb` 250–252). So:\n\n- Unisolated `MyEngine::Engine` → `my_engine_engine` (docs at `engine.rb` 176–184).\n- Isolated `isolate_namespace MyEngine` → `my_engine` (docs at `engine.rb` 249–252; asserted in `railties/test/railties/engine_test.rb` 952).\n- Nested `isolate_namespace Bukkits::Awesome` → engine name `bukkits_awesome`, default scope module `bukkits/awesome`.\n\n`isolated?` is an alias of `isolated` (`engine.rb` 354–355). Instances delegate `engine_name` and `isolated?` to the class (`engine.rb` 437). Default is `nil`/falsy until `isolate_namespace` runs.\n\nIf the module does **not** already respond to `railtie_namespace`, the same call defines these on the **module’s singleton class** (`engine.rb` 391–418):\n\n| Method | Effect |\n|---|---|\n| `railtie_namespace` | Returns the engine class that isolated it |\n| `table_name_prefix` | `\"#{engine_name}_\"`; after Active Record loads, `\"#{ActiveRecord::Base.table_name_prefix}#{engine_name}_\"` |\n| `use_relative_model_naming?` | `true` |\n| `railtie_helpers_paths` | `engine.helpers_paths` |\n| `railtie_routes_url_helpers` | `engine.routes.url_helpers(include_path_helpers)` |\n\nEach of those is skipped if the module already has the method. Isolating the same module twice therefore does **not** overwrite `railtie_namespace` (`engine_test.rb` 1228–1255). `engine_name`, `default_scope`, and `isolated = true` still apply to the engine that called `isolate_namespace`.\n\nThere are two different `railtie_namespace` methods:\n\n- Module method defined by isolation: `Bukkits.railtie_namespace` → `Bukkits::Engine` (`engine_test.rb` 953).\n- Railtie **instance** method: walk `self.class.module_parents` for a parent that responds to `railtie_namespace` (`railtie.rb` 302–304). After `Rails.application.isolate_namespace(AppTemplate)`, `Rails.application.railtie_namespace` is the module `AppTemplate` (`railties/test/application/multiple_applications_test.rb` 133–136).\n\n### Explicit `engine_name`\n\n`engine_name \"my_engine\"` is `railtie_name` assignment (`engine.rb` 184–191). Isolation itself assigns `engine_name` from `mod.name`, so a name set **before** `isolate_namespace` is overwritten. A name set **after** isolation is what `mount` sees, because `Mapper#mount` uses `app.railtie_name` at draw time (`actionpack/lib/action_dispatch/routing/mapper.rb` 639, 689–692).\n\n`table_name_prefix` closes over `name, railtie = engine_name, self` **at isolation time** (`engine.rb` 393–398). Later `engine_name` changes do not retarget the prefix. There is no engine/integration test that assigns `engine_name` explicitly; this is source behavior only.\n\n### Anonymous / unnamed namespaces\n\n`isolate_namespace` uses `mod.name` with no nil/anonymous guard (`engine.rb` 385–387). `Module.new` / other unnamed modules have `name == nil`. `Inflector.underscore(nil)` hits `Regexp#match?` and raises. There is no special-case test.\n\nAn unnamed **engine class** (`Class.new(Rails::Engine)`) is fine if isolation runs against a **named** module: `engine_name` is taken from `mod.name`, not `self.name`. Without isolation, `railtie_name` falls back to `generate_railtie_name(self.name)` and the same nil path applies (`railtie.rb` 180). The only anonymous-engine test is `routes?` being false before routes are materialized (`railties/test/engine_test.rb` 6–14).\n\n## 2. Table-name prefix and relative model naming\n\nIf the module already has `table_name_prefix`, isolation leaves it (`engine_test.rb` 1351–1366). Otherwise the prefix is `\"#{engine_name}_\"` and, once Active Record loads, `ActiveRecord::Base.table_name_prefix` is prepended (`engine.rb` 397–404; `engine_test.rb` 1369–1392: `\"ar_prefix_bukkits_\"` and table `ar_prefix_bukkits_posts`).\n\n`use_relative_model_naming?` makes `ActiveModel::Naming` pick that module as namespace (`activemodel/lib/active_model/naming.rb` 282–285). `ActiveModel::Name` then strips `\"#{namespace.name}::\"` for `param_key` / `route_key` (`naming.rb` 165–183). Externally:\n\n- `polymorphic_path(Bukkits::Post.new)` inside the engine is `/bukkits/posts/1`, not `/bukkits/bukkits_posts/1` (`engine_test.rb` 831–977, 905–906, 976–1032).\n- `form_for(Bukkits::Post.new)` emits `name=\"post[title]\"`, not `bukkits_post[title]` (`engine_test.rb` 1027–1032).\n- From the **application**, `polymorphic_path(Blog::Post.new)` is `/posts/44` (app routes), while `polymorphic_url([blog, Blog::Post.new])` is the mounted engine path (`railties/test/railties/mounted_engine_test.rb` 273–280).\n\n## 3. Route-set creation (still class/boot, not a request)\n\nDefault `config.route_set_class` is `ActionDispatch::Routing::RouteSet`; `config.default_scope` is `nil` (`railties/lib/rails/engine/configuration.rb` 47–48).\n\n`Engine#routes` lazily does `config.route_set_class.new_with_config(config)` (`engine.rb` 553–568). `RouteSet.new_with_config` copies `default_scope` onto the set (`actionpack/lib/action_dispatch/routing/route_set.rb` 375–388). `draw`/`eval_block` wraps the block in `mapper.with_default_scope(default_scope)` when present (`route_set.rb` 473–485; `mapper.rb` 655–658). That is `scope(module: \"bukkits\")` (or `\"bukkits/awesome\"`).\n\n`Mapping#add_controller_module` then prefixes the compiled controller (`mapper.rb` 329–338): `\"foo\"` + `\"bukkits\"` → `\"bukkits/foo\"`. A leading slash (`\"/foo\"`) strips the isolation module. Isolation does **not** add a path prefix or an `as:` prefix; only `module:`. That is why isolated `resources :posts` is still `/posts` on the engine and `posts_path`, while dispatch targets `Bukkits::PostsController` (`engine.rb` 226–237; `engine_test.rb` 1035–1071, 1074–1143).\n\n`routes?` is simply ` @routes` (`engine.rb` 706–707). Accessing `#routes` makes it true.\n\n## 4. Default `routes` vs custom `endpoint`\n\nClass `endpoint(app)` stores a Rack app (`engine.rb` 379–382). Instance `endpoint` is `self.class.endpoint || routes` (`engine.rb` 530–532). `app` builds the engine middleware stack around that endpoint (`engine.rb` 516–524). `call` always:\n\n```\nreq.routes = routes\nreq.engine_script_name = req.script_name\napp.call req.env\n```\n\n(`engine.rb` 535–538, 774–779). So a custom endpoint still publishes the engine’s route set in `env[\"action_dispatch.routes\"]` (`engine_test.rb` 800–828). With no custom endpoint, the route set **is** the Rack app (`engine_test.rb` 753–775). Custom endpoint + middleware: `engine_test.rb` 701–726.\n\n## 5. Engine with no route file\n\nInitializer `make_routes_lazy` **always** calls `routes`, creating the set (`engine.rb` 616–619). Then `add_routing_paths` (`engine.rb` 621–633):\n\n- Always copies `config/routes` draw paths onto engine and app.\n- Registers with `app.routes_reloader` only if `routes? || routing_paths.any?`.\n\nAfter `make_routes_lazy`, `routes?` is true, so an engine with no `config/routes.rb` still contributes an empty route set to the reloader. The reloader loads only existent files (`railties/lib/rails/application/routes_reloader.rb` 80–82). The unit test that `routes?` is false (`railties/test/engine_test.rb` 6–14) is pre-initializer: it forces `@routes = nil`.\n\n## 6. Lazy vs eager route loading\n\n`make_routes_lazy` sets `config.route_set_class = LazyRouteSet` when `Rails.env.local?` (`engine.rb` 616–618). That runs per engine and per application, so both get `LazyRouteSet` in local env.\n\n`Finisher#set_routes_reloader_hook` (`railties/lib/rails/application/finisher.rb` 160–176):\n\n```\nreloader.execute_unless_loaded if !app.routes.is_a?(Engine::LazyRouteSet) || app.config.eager_load\n```\n\n- Eager (`config.eager_load`, typically production): routes load at boot; `RoutesReloader#reload!` also `eager_load!`s each set (`routes_reloader.rb` 35).\n- Local + `LazyRouteSet`: boot leaves routes unloaded. First helper, `respond_to?(:*_path)`, request, `recognize_path`, `url_for`, or mounted-helper use calls `Rails.application.reload_routes_unless_loaded` (`railties/lib/rails/engine/lazy_route_set.rb`; `application.rb` 168–169).\n\n`LazyRouteSet` only treats `*_path`/`*_url` as lazy named helpers; other missing methods do not reload (`lazy_route_set.rb` 139–157; `railties/test/engine/lazy_route_set_test.rb` 45–58, 241–250). Mounted helpers (`plugin_engine`, `main_app`) reload on non-path names (`lazy_route_set.rb` 47–73, 219–273). Engine url helpers also lazy-load (`lazy_route_set_test.rb` 23–28, 78–86, 168–177). Concurrent first load waits on `RoutesReloader`’s monitor (`routes_reloader.rb` 45–73; `lazy_route_set_test.rb` 88–127).\n\n## 7. Mount helpers and `main_app`\n\n`mount` (`mapper.rb` 605–646):\n\n- Default `:as` for a Rails engine is `app.railtie_name` (the isolated `engine_name` unless overridden).\n- Draws the mount into the **parent** route set.\n- For a railtie, `define_generate_prefix` (`mapper.rb` 698–737):\n  - Builds a `script_namer` from the mount’s named route.\n  - `app.routes.define_mounted_helper(name, script_namer)` on the **engine** route set.\n  - Extends the engine route set so `find_script_name` uses that namer.\n\n`RouteSet#define_mounted_helper` defines `name` on the shared `MountedHelpers` module as a `RoutesProxy` (`route_set.rb` 508–536; `actionpack/lib/action_dispatch/routing/routes_proxy.rb`). First definition of a given name wins.\n\n`main_app` is **not** created by isolation. `Finisher#define_main_app_helper` does `app.routes.define_mounted_helper(:main_app)` on the **application** route set (`finisher.rb` 60–62).\n\nAction Controller (and Action Mailer) then:\n\n- `include app.routes.mounted_helpers` — application’s proxies: `main_app`, `bukkits`, `blog`, …\n- `extend RoutesHelpers.with(app.routes)` — on subclassing, include **either** the isolated namespace’s `railtie_routes_url_helpers` **or** the app route helpers (`actionpack/lib/abstract_controller/railties/routes_helpers.rb` 9–18; `actionpack/lib/action_controller/railtie.rb` 81–82; `actionmailer/lib/action_mailer/railtie.rb` 41–42).\n\nThat split is the externally visible helper isolation:\n\n- Isolated `Bukkits::FooController` has `foo_path`, not `bar_path` or app helpers (`engine_test.rb` 831–974).\n- Same controller has `main_app.bar_path` (`engine_test.rb` 905–906, 971–972).\n- Isolated mailer has `foo_url`, not `bar_url` (`engine_test.rb` 954–955).\n- App controller uses `bukkits.bukkit_path`; engine controller uses `main_app.bar_path`; `SCRIPT_NAME` / `relative_url_root` compose (`engine_test.rb` 1656–1768).\n- One isolated engine’s helpers are not another’s: `metrics.respond_to?(:weblogs_path)` is false (`mounted_engine_test.rb` 312–315).\n- Nested isolated engines: `bukkits_awesome.bar_path` from the parent isolated engine (`engine_test.rb` 1074–1143).\n- Multiple mounts: distinct `as:` helpers (`fruit_bukkits` / `vegetable_bukkits`) and dynamic `script_namer` (`engine_test.rb` 1770–1862).\n- Non-isolated engine mounted at `/` still gets a mount helper named from `railtie_name` (`mounted_engine_test.rb` 294–301).\n\n`prepend_helpers_path` does **not** copy an isolated engine’s helper dir onto the app unless `app == self` (`engine.rb` 665–668). Controller helper lookup uses `railtie_helpers_paths` on the isolated module (`actionpack/lib/action_controller/railties/helpers.rb` 12–16). Isolated controllers see engine helpers (`help_the_engine`) and not app helpers (`something`) (`engine_test.rb` 831–969). `Engine#helpers` still gathers the engine’s helper modules for opt-in inclusion (`engine.rb` 502–509; `engine_test.rb` 1413–1450).\n\n## 8. Request-time dispatch (controller lookup)\n\nIsolation does not install a custom dispatcher. At request time:\n\n1. Parent router matches `mount` and calls the engine (`Engine#call`).\n2. `build_request` sets `req.routes` to the **engine** route set and `engine_script_name` (`engine.rb` 774–779).\n3. Engine endpoint (routes or custom) runs.\n4. Engine router matches e.g. `controller: \"bukkits/foo\"` — already compiled with `default_scope`.\n5. `Request#controller_class_for` does `name.underscore.camelize + \"Controller\"` then `constantize` (`actionpack/lib/action_dispatch/http/request.rb` 85–108). `\"bukkits/foo\"` → `Bukkits::FooController`. `\"bukkits/awesome/foo\"` → `Bukkits::Awesome::FooController` (`engine_test.rb` 1035–1071).\n\nNo extra namespace walk happens at dispatch. If the route was compiled without the module prefix (non-isolated engine, or `to: \"/foo#index\"`), constantize looks for `FooController` at the root.\n\nMount order is application-visible: a more specific app route should be declared **before** `mount`, or the engine sees the request first (`engine.rb` 157–174).\n\n## Phase split\n\n```text\nCLASS BODY\n  isolate_namespace(Mod)\n    engine_name = underscore(Mod.name).tr(\"/\", \"_\")\n    isolated = true\n    config.default_scope = { module: underscore(Mod.name) }\n    define on Mod (first caller only):\n      railtie_namespace, table_name_prefix,\n      use_relative_model_naming?, railtie_helpers_paths,\n      railtie_routes_url_helpers\n\nBOOT\n  make_routes_lazy → route_set_class, Engine#routes (empty set)\n  add_routing_paths → reloader.paths / route_sets\n  define_main_app_helper → MountedHelpers#main_app\n  eager? execute_unless_loaded : leave LazyRouteSet unloaded\n  AC/AM on_load → mounted_helpers + RoutesHelpers\n\nROUTE DRAW (boot or first lazy access)\n  load config/routes.rb*\n  engine draw → with_default_scope(module:)\n  app mount → default as: engine_name, define_mounted_helper + script_namer\n\nREQUEST\n  app router → Engine#call → engine router\n  controller = path_parameters[:controller].camelize + \"Controller\"\n  helpers already chosen at controller class inherit\n```\n\nIsolation’s routing effect is almost entirely the compile-time `default_scope` plus helper/module wiring. Request dispatch only consumes the controller string those routes already contain."
}
```

## Assistant

`isolate_namespace` is class-level setup. It does not dispatch requests. It stamps the engine and the passed module so later boot, route compile, helper inclusion, and request dispatch behave as an isolated app. Mounting and `call` are a later phase.

### Class-level setup

[`Rails::Engine.isolate_namespace`](railties/lib/rails/engine.rb) (lines 384–419) always:

1. Sets `engine_name` from the **module** name, not the engine class: `engine_name(generate_railtie_name(mod.name))`.
2. Sets `config.default_scope = { module: ActiveSupport::Inflector.underscore(mod.name) }`.
3. Sets `self.isolated = true`.

`engine_name` is an alias of `railtie_name`. `generate_railtie_name` is `underscore(string).tr("/", "_")`. So unisolated `MyEngine::Engine` is `my_engine_engine`; isolated `isolate_namespace MyEngine` is `my_engine` (asserted in [`engine_test.rb`](railties/test/railties/engine_test.rb) line 952). Nested `Bukkits::Awesome` becomes engine name `bukkits_awesome` and default scope module `bukkits/awesome`.

If the module does not already respond to `railtie_namespace`, isolation defines on its singleton class:

- `railtie_namespace` → the engine class
- `table_name_prefix` → `"#{engine_name}_"`, later `"#{ActiveRecord::Base.table_name_prefix}#{engine_name}_"`
- `use_relative_model_naming?` → `true`
- `railtie_helpers_paths` / `railtie_routes_url_helpers` → that engine’s helpers and `routes.url_helpers`

Each definition is skipped if the method already exists. Isolating the same module twice does not overwrite `railtie_namespace` ([`engine_test.rb`](railties/test/railties/engine_test.rb) 1228–1255). `engine_name`, `default_scope`, and `isolated = true` still apply to the engine that called it.

**Explicit `engine_name`:** assignment is `railtie_name=`. Isolation overwrites a name set *before* it. A name set *after* is what `mount` sees (`app.railtie_name` at draw time). `table_name_prefix` closes over `engine_name` at isolation time, so a later rename does not retarget the prefix. No engine/integration test assigns `engine_name` explicitly.

**Anonymous / unnamed namespaces:** `isolate_namespace` uses `mod.name` with no nil guard. Unnamed modules (`name == nil`) blow up in `Inflector.underscore`. An unnamed *engine class* is fine if isolation runs against a named module. The only anonymous-engine test is [`railties/test/engine_test.rb`](railties/test/engine_test.rb) 6–14, which checks `routes?` is false before routes are materialized.

### Table-name prefix and relative naming

Existing `table_name_prefix` is left alone. Otherwise Active Record prepends `ActiveRecord::Base.table_name_prefix` (`"ar_prefix_bukkits_posts"` in [`engine_test.rb`](railties/test/railties/engine_test.rb) 1369–1392).

`use_relative_model_naming?` makes `ActiveModel::Naming` strip the isolated module from `param_key` / `route_key`. Externally: `polymorphic_path(Bukkits::Post.new)` inside the engine is `/bukkits/posts/1`; `form_for` emits `name="post[title]"`. From the application, `polymorphic_path(Blog::Post.new)` uses app routes; `polymorphic_url([blog, Blog::Post.new])` uses the mount ([`mounted_engine_test.rb`](railties/test/railties/mounted_engine_test.rb) 273–280).

### Route-set creation

`Engine#routes` lazily does `config.route_set_class.new_with_config(config)`, which copies `default_scope`. `draw` wraps the block in `scope(module: "bukkits")`. `Mapping#add_controller_module` prefixes the compiled controller (`"foo"` → `"bukkits/foo"`). A leading slash (`"/foo"`) strips isolation. Isolation does **not** add a path or `as:` prefix, which is why isolated `resources :posts` is still `/posts` and `posts_path` while dispatch targets `Bukkits::PostsController`.

### Default `routes` vs custom `endpoint`

Instance `endpoint` is `self.class.endpoint || routes`. `call` always sets `req.routes = routes` and `req.engine_script_name`, then runs the middleware stack around that endpoint. A custom endpoint still publishes the engine route set in `env["action_dispatch.routes"]`.

### Engine with no route file

Initializer `make_routes_lazy` always calls `routes`, creating the set. `add_routing_paths` then registers with `app.routes_reloader` if `routes? || routing_paths.any?`. After that initializer, `routes?` is true, so a missing `config/routes.rb` still contributes an empty set. The reloader only `load`s existent files. The unit test that `routes?` is false is pre-initializer.

### Lazy vs eager loading

`make_routes_lazy` sets `route_set_class = LazyRouteSet` when `Rails.env.local?`. Finisher then:

```
reloader.execute_unless_loaded if !app.routes.is_a?(Engine::LazyRouteSet) || app.config.eager_load
```

Eager (typically production): routes load at boot. Local + `LazyRouteSet`: first helper, `respond_to?(:*_path)`, request, `recognize_path`, `url_for`, or mounted-helper use calls `reload_routes_unless_loaded`. Only `*_path`/`*_url` (and non-path mounted helper names) trigger the load; other missing methods do not ([`lazy_route_set_test.rb`](railties/test/engine/lazy_route_set_test.rb)).

### Mount helpers and `main_app`

`mount` defaults `:as` to `app.railtie_name`, draws into the parent set, and for a railtie defines a `script_namer` plus `define_mounted_helper` on the **engine** route set. `main_app` is **not** created by isolation; Finisher defines it on the **application** route set.

Action Controller/Mailer then include the app’s `mounted_helpers` and, on subclassing, either the isolated module’s `railtie_routes_url_helpers` or the app helpers. Isolated controllers get `foo_path` and `main_app.bar_path`, not app helpers. Isolated engines do not share each other’s mount helpers. Nested engines get `bukkits_awesome.bar_path`. Multiple mounts use distinct `as:` names and dynamic `script_namer`.

`prepend_helpers_path` does not copy an isolated engine’s helper dir onto the app. Controller helper lookup uses `railtie_helpers_paths` on the isolated module.

### Request-time dispatch

Isolation does not install a custom dispatcher. The parent router matches `mount`, `Engine#call` stamps the engine route set on the request, the engine router matches the already-prefixed `controller: "bukkits/foo"`, and `Request#controller_class_for` constantizes `"bukkits/foo".camelize + "Controller"` → `Bukkits::FooController`. No extra namespace walk happens at dispatch.

```text
CLASS BODY
  isolate_namespace(Mod)
    engine_name = underscore(Mod.name).tr("/", "_")
    isolated = true
    config.default_scope = { module: underscore(Mod.name) }
    define on Mod (first caller only):
      railtie_namespace, table_name_prefix,
      use_relative_model_naming?, railtie_helpers_paths,
      railtie_routes_url_helpers

BOOT
  make_routes_lazy → route_set_class, Engine#routes
  add_routing_paths → reloader.paths / route_sets
  define_main_app_helper → MountedHelpers#main_app
  eager? execute_unless_loaded : leave LazyRouteSet unloaded
  AC/AM on_load → mounted_helpers + RoutesHelpers

ROUTE DRAW (boot or first lazy access)
  engine draw → with_default_scope(module:)
  app mount → default as: engine_name, define_mounted_helper + script_namer

REQUEST
  app router → Engine#call → engine router
  controller = path_parameters[:controller].camelize + "Controller"
  helpers already chosen at controller class inherit
```

Isolation’s routing effect is almost entirely the compile-time `default_scope` plus helper/module wiring. Request dispatch only consumes the controller string those routes already contain.
